Property build — every chunk, every task, status

As of 2026-09-25. One page for the whole plan: the original 15 chunks (0–14, closed 2026-09-24) and the remaining build, chunks 15–28 (planned 2026-09-25; chunks 15 and 16 gated 2026-09-25). Sources, and what to edit when something changes: CF-START-HERE.md (resume point), CF-PROPERTY-BUILD-ORDER.md (the plan and the gate), CF-CHUNK2-TASKS.md through CF-CHUNK11-TASKS.md (the task lists), CF-CHUNK1-GATE.md through CF-CHUNK10-GATE.md (the filed gates), CF-ENGINE-FIX-BANDED-LOOKUP.md (chunk 10's engine fix).
Chunks 0–11 are CLOSED (chunk 11, Leasehold Interest, closed 2026-09-22: gated 9 of 9, 24 of 24, 4 user-authorized engine fixes, confirmed against ISO live). Chunk 12 (the endorsement families) OPENED 2026-09-22; GATED 2026-09-24, 42 of 42 (CF-CHUNK12-GATE.md; Groups A–G closed, all six families ISO-confirmed; regression CLEAN for chunks 0–12; left: file the gate; Debris Removal, Ordinance or Law, Utility Services, Unmanned Aircraft and Green Upgrades each gated 9 of 9 and ISO-confirmed live; OI-109/110 fixed (a missing value is skipped in a premium product, as at ISO, and recorded); OI-108, OI-109 and OI-110 confirmed live and fixed). Verify the whole build in one command: python scripts/cf/104_chunk10_c10-21_regression.py (covers chunks 0–12 since 2026-09-23).
* on a gate cell means passed, but re-tested later under a stronger check — see the note below.

Chunk 10 — Earthquake, OPENED AND GATED 9 of 9 ON 2026-09-22, 21 of 21 tasks done, 1 engine gap found AND FIXED same day. Task list at CF-CHUNK10-TASKS.md (C10-01–C10-21), gate at CF-CHUNK10-GATE.md. The real reframe: the 97 *Earthquake*-named files are NOT the rating engine — the actual chain lives inside the same five parent files chunks 1–9 already opened. C10-01 (golden case) closed clean: $6 EQ premium, $261 ordinary unmoved, no engine gap. Gate Groups 1 and 2 closed the remaining 8 gate points the same day, all clean — gate: 9 of 9, no engine gap on any gate point. Gate Group 3 (C10-10–14) verified those claims across all 5 groups: Contents $5, Business Income $5, Special Class $6, SC Business Income $5, all exact; 2 new ancestor-copy traps found on Contents. Gate Group 4 (C10-15–18) closed the reachability map: EQ's own blanket branch ($6/$12, and ISO itself refuses a 1-structure version); Legal Liability's 9-file EQ branch ($2 EQ, chunk 8's $60 unmoved); Non-Owned Trailers (found to attach under CONTENTS, not Building, $5, its own real $5,000 threshold); Special Class's 27 files (8 core + 19 wrapper across two sibling groups). Gate Group 5 (C10-19) then found this chunk's first real engine gap: the plain flat-dollar-deductible form (CP 10 28) works and rates $8, genuinely distinct from the percentage path's $6 — but the SUB-LIMIT flat-dollar form (CP 10 29) was initially unreachable: all 13 EQSubLimit*FlatDeductibleFactor tables carry a double-banded range key the interpreter's own _lookup_banded refused outright. Fixed the same day, user-authorized — _lookup_banded now handles any number of key ranges (one interpolates, named by the table's own RangeKeyCol; every other is a plain bracket filter). A corpus-wide check found this wasn't Earthquake-only: 28 CF tables carry two ranges — the 13 EQ sub-limit tables, 7 live Flood rate-modification tables, 1 live Massachusetts heating-oil table, and 7 confirmed-dead tables left untouched. The fixed CP 10 29 case now runs to $6, hand-derived and matched. Full regression: 16 of 16 GL suites, 107 of 120 CF scripts (the other 13 pre-existing/unrelated failures, not caused by this change). Write-up: CF-ENGINE-FIX-BANDED-LOOKUP.md. C10-21 closed 2026-09-22 — the chunks 1–9 regression is CLEAN under that fix (all 10 golden cases reproduce every documented figure, 25 machinery scripts and 16 GL suites green), run as a reproducible self-testing harness rather than a hand sweep. C10-20 closed the same day and corrected the opening census's own fixture arithmetic in three places — the "924" denominator is two lines of business (560 GL + 364 CF), "69" counts Structures rather than payloads (149 electing records in total), and EQ's blanket branch has zero ISO fixtures. CHUNK 10 IS CLOSED: 21 of 21.
Chunk 9 — Blanket rating, CLOSED 22 of 22 on 2026-09-21. Gated 9 of 9, all 22 tasks numbered C9-01–C9-22 in CF-CHUNK9-TASKS.md done. The opening census overturned the build order's own premise — “Branch B is a stub in every premium file” did not survive a direct read: zero of the 20 premium files chunks 1–8 built stub the blanket formula out. Every one carries a complete, already-filed formula (IRPMFactor × PackageModFactor × Blkt<Form>AvgRate × (item value / BlktTotalFullValueAmount) × (BlktLimit/100)). What was missing was the input, and C9-01's golden case found exactly why: ForEach AtInputDataDef in gl_engine/interp/nodes.py reparented WRITES onto the iterated Detail record along with reads, so SetNonEQBlanketValues's output never reached the structure it was meant for. Fixed 2026-09-21, the first engine change since chunk 0 — Frame gained a write_root that keeps a bare ToDataDef pinned to the frame active before an AtInputDataDef loop, while AtDataDef loops keep rebinding both as before. Golden case matches hand-derivation exactly, $255 / $510, and C9-13 confirmed it live against ISO to the dollar, all 3 validation messages included — the first CF chunk to do both at once for a submission absent from ISO's own fixture corpus. C9-15 through C9-22 then closed out every remaining task: all 3 of Building's forms activate blanket on a real run, no operand-order divergence exists anywhere in the 20 formulas, MultiPremiumAndDispersionCreditFactor is live on 12 of 20 and genuinely absent (not dead) on the other 8, the IRPM/PackageMod level count varies by group (5/7/9, not uniformly 5), cross-coverage isolation is clean, the 3 shared instruments now cover a 7th DataDefGroup, and every chunk 1–8 golden case plus all 16 GL suites re-ran green after the interpreter fix. Census: scripts/cf/67_chunk9_census.py. Golden case: CF-CHUNK9-GOLDEN-CASE.md. Gate: CF-CHUNK9-GATE.md. Full task list: CF-CHUNK9-TASKS.md.
Chunk 5 is gated at 8 of 9: all 28 tasks closed, four forms rating, 44 of 47 jurisdictions exact, 6 ISO calls all matching, and no engine code written — the fifth coverage in a row on chunk 0's foundation. Chunk 4 is gated at 8 of 9: all 32 tasks closed, four Contents forms rating, 46 of 47 jurisdictions, 6 ISO calls all matching, and no engine code written — the fourth coverage in a row on chunk 0's foundation. Chunk 3 is gated at 8 of 9 as of 2026-09-18, with all 24 of its tasks closed and no engine code written — the third coverage in a row to run on chunk 0's foundation unchanged. Broad $16 and Special $54 ISO-confirmed, 47 of 47 jurisdictions with nothing skipped, 7 further ISO calls all matching. Gate point 9 (validation messages) is the one outstanding point, and it is owed on chunks 1 and 2 equally. Full gate: CF-CHUNK3-GATE.md.
Gate point 6's check was blind, and chunks 1 and 2 passed under it. 04_shared_names.py collected only the rate tables named directly inside a rule — but ISO's Set* rules delegate their lookups via RunRule, so the two documented trap cases, defined in 16 and 6 datadef groups, each reported zero tables and could never have been flagged. Following RunRule transitively takes the chunk 1 chain from 1 ambiguous name to 6, chunk 2 from 1 to 4, chunk 3 from 1 to 9, and the corpus from 9 to 83. All nineteen are now adjudicated and matching — but those two gates were passed on a weaker check than the one they now stand on. Third time a defect has been found in the instrument rather than the corpus. Full write-up: CF-CHUNK3-SHARED-NAMES.md.
The sweep's finding: Florida and Puerto Rico rate Special by their own rule. The first pass skipped both as “no rate filed”, because the countrywide table name finds no row there — and the engine rated Florida at $38 anyway. Both override SetSpecialBaseRate to read a differently-named table: FL splits residential / non-residential on 21 hardcoded class codes; PR gates on "Special Including Theft" / "Special Excluding Theft", two cause-of-loss values that do not exist countrywide, and keys the rate on construction type. A submission saying Special is off-domain in Puerto Rico and prices at zero in silence. Full write-up: CF-CHUNK3-STATE-SWEEP.md.
A construction type that is spelled right and prices at zero. ConstructionType is a 25-value submission domain that maps onto the six rating types, and Frame is the only one of the six that is also a valid input. Submitting Joisted Masonry — the name the rate table itself is keyed on — misses the domain lookup and prices the whole Broad chain at zero with nothing raised. The eighth member of the absent-means-zero family, and the first where the input is present, correctly spelled and semantically right. Full write-up: CF-CHUNK3-BROAD-STRUCTURE.md.
Two zero-case predictions were wrong, and both are worth carrying. Special's deductible fall-through was documented as having no Otherwise branch; the filed XML has one, so a blank deductible zeroes Special exactly as it zeroes Broad — a defect in our own notes, now corrected in CauseOfLoss_Building_RatingAlgorithms.md. And a missing Limit does not price at zero: the null-premium guard refuses the submission. That makes it the only one of seven known missing inputs that fails loudly. Full write-up: CF-CHUNK3-ZERO-CASES.md.
ISO refused four Contents payloads that our engine rates. Every submission without a WindstormOrHailDeductible came back Matrix: WindstormOrHailDeductibleAsNumber … No results have been returned. — and our engine prices it happily. That is a fresh instance of the open SingleResult-with-zero-hits question, where chunk 2 measured blanket strictness and disproved it because it broke VA and VT, which ISO rates. This is the first same-corpus pair of a refusal and a tolerance, and the best material yet for the targeted probes that open item needs.
Three instrument defects in two days, and none in the corpus. Gate point 6's check was blind to delegating rules; gate point 7's census was blind to theft; the state sweep's first pass was blind to rule overrides. Each returned a clean result that was really an empty search. The habit that caught all three is the same: predict a specific number, and treat the disagreement as the finding — including when the disagreement is with our own notes.
Puerto Rico can never be confirmed against ISO, and it is the state that most needs it. The RAaS subscription does not cover PR (OI-86, closed 2026-08-13) — and PR is the jurisdiction with the most unusual Special reading in the corpus: its own cause-of-loss domain, its own construction-keyed rate table, its own theft rule, its own Broad base rate and its own LOI table. Every PR premium rests on a reading of the filed rules and nothing else, and that has to be said wherever a PR premium is presented.
Committed (checked 2026-09-23). cf_engine/, the chunk scripts in scripts/cf/ and the docs/CF-* files are in git (last: 0c11c9c, chunks 9–11). This note said “nothing is committed” until 2026-09-23, which had been stale since those commits. Chunk 12's Group A files are new and not yet committed.

4 Contents (Personal Property) — all four forms Gate 8 of 9 — all 32 tasks

Built differentially against chunks 1–3, and gated 2026-09-18 with no engine code written — the fourth coverage in a row on chunk 0's foundation. Golden cases: Basic Group I $223, Group II $24, Broad $16, Special $137; Building unmoved at $263/$32. 32 tasks numbered C4-01–C4-32; the nine gate-level ones are closed and all 32 tasks are now closed rather than gate conditions.

Three things that make it different in kind

 Why it is new, not just different data
It rates a collectionN Personal Property records per Occupancy Class per Location, driven by a ForEach. The first repeating group in the project — and ISO sums it the way we do (209 + 105, confirmed)
Four fields are inherited, not per-itemCovForm was known; BldgClassCode, RatingType and OccupCategoryRiskSeverity were not. All are Copyd from an ancestor, and a submission that sets them per item is silently overruled
Special is the opposite of Building'sBuilding's is a flat statewide constant. PP's is keyed on occupancy category and severity, carries its own territory multiplier, and has two protective-safeguard credits no Building form has. 14 steps against 9

The state layer again, wider than chunk 2's Every PP base-rate table is header-only countrywide: BasicGroupIRate (44 of 47 — AK, CA and PR file it in no edition at all), BasicGroupIIRate and SpecialPrsnlPropRate (45 of 47, missing FL and PR), PrsnlPropTerrMultiplier and SpecialPrsnlPropTheftExclusionFactor (46 of 47, missing PR).

The collection problem — new machinery

 #Task
✅10Two PP records under one Occupancy Class both rate and sum — $209 + $105 = $314, ISO-confirmed. The LOI factor is the OCCUPANCY's, not the item's: both take 0.842 off a 150,000 occupancy total, so two small items do not price as one large one
✅11No leakage — two items differing only in CovType get their own vault factors (1.0 / 0.5) and rates. The first draft varied the class code and produced a perfect false positive, because that field is inherited
✅12Inherited fields — and there are four, not one: CovForm, BldgClassCode, RatingType and OccupCategoryRiskSeverity. A per-item value is accepted and silently discarded
✅13The two Special sums, separated at last. CP 10 33 on the structure drives them apart (200,000 vs 100,000) while leaving branch 1 able to fire, and with a 2,500 theft deductible the factor is 0.89 not 0.92 and the premium $125 not $129. Four dollars is the entire observable difference between the two sums — and the household-limit route the rules suggested turned out to be unreachable

Carried in from chunk 3, and live here A submission can populate a derived field (task 27 is the first place ISO intends it; everywhere else it is a hole) · ConstructionType is a 25-value domain, not the six rating names · SetSpecialBaseRate reads five different tables and PP's is SpecialPrsnlPropRate · class 1150 has at least two independent consequences · PR has no ISO confirmation and never will, and is the only jurisdiction missing every PP base-rate table.

Deliberately not in chunk 4 Earthquake (chunk 10, and PP has its own EQ chain) · Legal Liability (chunk 8), though the PP premium files carry its branch · blanket (chunk 9, still a stub) · Business Income (chunk 5) · theft limitations, sublimits, agreed value, inflation guard and vacancy — endorsements, chunk 12.

5 Business Income — the Factor chain and four forms Gate 8 of 9 — all 28 tasks

Opened 2026-09-18, 28 tasks numbered C5-01–C5-28 in CF-CHUNK5-TASKS.md, derived from the algorithms doc and from censuses run while opening the chunk.

Four things that make it different in kind

 Why it is new, not just different data
It is arithmetically DOWNSTREAM of BuildingBasic Group I, Basic Group II and Earthquake have no base rate of their own — they read Building's already-computed rate, symbol and territory from two to four levels up and apply a small adjustment factor. The first coverage in the project that cannot be rated without rating another coverage first, so every chunk 1–3 finding is now load-bearing twice
SetFactor picks exactly ONE of fourMax period, monthly limit, extra expense, or type of risk — in priority order. Multiplying them instead of selecting would overstate the premium by a large multiple, and a single case cannot show which happened. Broad and Special never apply it at all
Four Building concepts are simply absentNo deductible factor anywhere (the "deductible" is a waiting period built into the rate), no limit-of-insurance factor (the premium multiplies Limit / 100 directly), no multi-premium credit despite the datadef being populated, and two premium branches instead of four
Add-ons charge a surcharge, not a rateAgreed Value and Extended Period of Indemnity compute (Factor − 1) × Rate × Limit/100 — only the incremental load. Nothing in Building or Contents uses that shape

The state layer — quiet, for the first time since chunk 1 BusnIncomeFactor carries 131 countrywide rows and resolves in 47 of 47, as do the monthly-limit (60), Broad base (30), Special base (24), max-period (22) and extra-expense (4) tables. Only BaseRateAdjustmentFactor is header-only — 46 of 47 file it, PR does not. Rule overrides are just as quiet: SetFactor, SetTypeOfRiskFactor, SetBroadBaseRate and SetCoinsuranceToUse are overridden by 0 of 47. The one outlier is SetBasicGroupIICauseOfLossAdjustment at 10 of 47 — AR, DC, FL, IN, KS, KY, NY, PR, RI, TX. So chunk 5 inverts chunk 2 and chunk 4's problem: the tables are there, and what is unsettled is whether the countrywide chain holds.

The Factor — the chunk's central machinery

 #Task
✅10Exactly one, by priority — and the three-way case was predicted at $233 and came back $0. The winning branch is itself keyed on CovType, cannot resolve, and SetFactor does not fall through. ✅11All three keys move it — Manufacturing 0.71 → $144, Without Extra Expense 0.50 → $102, coinsurance 80% 0.59 → $120. And the direction is inverted against Building: a lower coinsurance percentage is a higher factor, because this is a discount table
✅12Neither table exists — they are datadefs read from the SUBMISSION. The blend weights two submitted component factors by two submitted percentages, all four through FromConstant="0.0", so absent means zero on every one. 40%/60% at 0.71/0.55 → 0.614 → $125. A combined risk is a selection plus four submitted numbers — which is why the filed row is 0 and C5-03's case priced at zero

6 Special Class — building side Gate 8 of 9 — all 29 tasks

Opened 2026-09-18, 29 tasks numbered C6-01–C6-29 in CF-CHUNK6-TASKS.md, derived from the algorithms doc and from the census run while opening the chunk (scripts/cf/40_chunk6_census.py).

Five things that make it different in kind

 Why it is new, not just different data
1The rating driver is a free-text class DESCRIPTION, not a class code. Basic Group I's base rate keys on SpecialClassDescConvertedOption — itself the result of mapping a 100-character English string through a second table to one of 71 OptionNN codes. Two lookups in series, the first on prose. No ConstructionCode key at all
2Protection class is a KEY, not a factor. It is folded into the Group I base-rate lookup. ProtectionClassFactor is still computed — last, after every chain has run — and then never read. The first orphaned factor computed after the point where it could have been used
3Nine of Building's rating concepts are simply absent — LOI factor, roof surfacing (ACV and cosmetic), BCEG, sprinkler leakage, the substandard and vacant addends, MultiResidentialPropSpecialCreditFactor, the CovType taxonomy and the Legal Liability branch. Every premium file has two branches: blanket or scheduled
4Basic Group I and II are never gated on the cause of loss. SetCoverageOnPolicyIndicator is hard-coded to 1 in both. The pattern that has held for three chunks running is half false here — exactly the shape a fourth chunk would inherit without checking
5Broad's base rate is construction-blind — it keys on the hard-coded literal "Frame". Building's reads the item's actual construction and moves 0.015 → 0.010. A fire-resistive Special Class item pays the frame rate

The state layer — the loudest since chunk 2 Three tables are header-only countrywide — BasicGroupIIRate, LowestBasicGroupIIRate and SpecialBuildingRate, the last of these the same unfilled table chunk 3 found on Building. Group I's own BasicGroupIRateSpecialClass carries 213 countrywide rows and only FL (204), NY (189) and PR (213) file their own; the two class-description converters carry 71 each, and FL files 68 while NY files a header-only table. Deductible250Factor is declared by 0 of 47, and BasicGroupIIFlatCoinsuranceFactor is 1.5 — a surcharge, where every other coinsurance factor in the project runs at or below 1.0. The eight states filing no LowestBasicGroupIIRate — AR, DC, FL, KS, NY, PR, RI, TX — are all inside the eleven that override the rule which reads it.

Rule deletions, on rating rules, in five places. Chunk 5 found the first rating rule any state empties to <rul:Sequence />. Chunk 6 finds five more: SetSpecialClassDescConvertedOption (NY), SetBasicGroupIISymbolNum (NY, PR), SetBasicGroupIINumericValue (NY), SetSpecialTheftExclusionFactor (PR) and SetProtectionClassFactor (PR). Ordinary override traffic elsewhere: SetBasicGroupIICauseOfLossAdjustment by 11 — the same shape chunk 5 found at 10.

Five things that make it different in kind

 Why it is new, not just different data
1The first coverage that borrows a FULLY RATED rate. Business Income reads the Building's BasicGroupIBaseRate — before territory, protection class and the addends — and rebuilds a parallel formula. This one reads ../../BasicGroupIRate, the Special Class item's finished rate, and adds nothing at the rate step. SIX values cross the boundary — the opening census said five and C7-01 found the sixth: two finished rates copied outright (BasicGroupIRate, EQRate), three inputs feeding its own lookups (BasicGroupIISymbol, BasicGroupIIRatingTerr, EQTerr), and BasicGroupIINumericValue, the parent’s multiplier of 8, read outright. No BasicGroupIBaseRate anywhere
2Two forms are keyed on literals, and one blanks a dimension the same table still uses. Broad always reads the "Frame" row; Special always reads "Other than Apartments and Condominiums". So the apartment rows are permanently unreachable from this group — while plain Business Income reaches them on the same physical table. Corrected 2026-09-18: the algorithms page says the theft factor two steps earlier still varies by ApartmentCondoIndicator. It does not — that lookup is pinned to "No" from every site. Both dimensions are dead
3Fewer multipliers than either parent, and the census measures it. StdPropPolGroupIFactor appears 0 times here against 5 in Special Class Building; BaseRateAdjustmentFactor, BuildersRiskFactor, LOIFactor and MultiResidentialPropSpecialCreditFactor are 0 in both. Basic Group I's whole cause-of-loss adjustment is rate × VandalismExclFactor — one multiplier
4Earthquake is a real fifth form with no standalone premium file — it borrows EQRate wholesale and charges only through Agreed Value. Chunk 5 found the same asymmetry on plain Business Income; two instances make it a pattern
525 of 47 jurisdictions file their own rules file for this group — the highest state participation of any chunk so far

The state layer BasicGroupIIRate and LowestBasicGroupIIRate are header-only countrywide and declared by 45 and 39 states, so Group II is unresolvable at CW for the third coverage group running. Only FL files its own BusnIncomeFactor, max-period, monthly-limit and extra-expense tables; only PR files its own Broad base rate and leasehold theft factor. Two tables have no consumer in this group at all: SpecialFormBldrsRiskTimeElementBaseRate (no 1150 branch to reach it) and BaseRateAdjustmentFactor — which is plain Business Income's table, filed by 46 of 47 jurisdictions and named zero times in this ruleset. Rule deletions again: SetLowestBasicGroupIIBaseRate is emptied to <rul:Sequence /> by six states — AR, DC, KS, NY, RI, TX — which are six of the eight filing no lowest-rate table, so C6-19's biconditional gets re-asked on a second coverage. SetSpecialTheftExclusionFactor is deleted by PR exactly as in chunk 6. Nothing at all overrides SetBasicGroupIRate, SetFactor, the whole Broad chain or SetEQRate.

The borrowed rate — the chunk's central machinery

 #Task
✅10The borrow, proven by moving it. 8 Legal Liability — Building and Contents Gate 7 of 9 — all 26 tasks

Opened 2026-09-18, 26 tasks numbered C8-01–C8-26 in CF-CHUNK8-TASKS.md, derived from the two traced comparison pages and from the census run while opening the chunk (scripts/cf/58_chunk8_census.py).

Five things that make it different in kind

 Why it is new, not just different data
1The first chunk where the divergence is already traced. Chunks 4–7 opened by discovering a shape; this one opens with an eleven-point map in hand. So the first job is not to discover it but to check it — exactly as C7-27 checked the Special Class Business Income reuse map. That check found our error last time; it may find the map's this time
2The fork is in SetPremium, not the rate chain — and it starts three steps early. Everything through BasicGroupICauseOfLossAdjustment is shared rule for rule. Then coinsurance, the LOI factor and the deductible are computed and discarded, the premium starts from the cause-of-loss adjustment instead of the final rate, and MultiPremiumAndDispersionCreditFactor is not applied. No chunk so far has had a path that throws away work it has already done
3Contents is double. One two-row table, keyed "Bldg" → 0.25 and "PersProp" → 0.50. One table, one key, twice the money
4The two sides gate on different fields at different LEVELS. Building tests CovType on the item; Contents tests CovForm inherited from the occupancy class. One Contents item cannot carry a different answer from its siblings; a Building can
5Blanket is unreachable by two different mechanisms. Building reaches blanket by elimination and Legal Liability never gets there; Contents tests IncludedInBlkt = "Yes" explicitly and excludes the Legal Liability form by name. Same outcome, two reasons

The state layer — the quietest of any chunk Not one jurisdiction files its own copy of any of the three tables this coverage owns: LegalLiabilityFactor (2 countrywide rows — Bldg 0.25, PersProp 0.50), AddlInsurableInterestFactor (4 rows — 1.25 / 1.25 / 1.0 / 1.0) and AdditionalInsurableIntOption (4 rows). After six chunks of state overrides this is the first coverage whose entire distinctive data is countrywide-only. And only two jurisdictions rewrite any of the eight premium files — FL on both Group II files, PR on both Special files, nobody on the other four. Branch ordering checked in all 47 rather than read once: Legal Liability sits at branch 2 in all eight countrywide files and in all four state copies, 0 files where blanket comes first — the map's central structural argument survives the check. One line of the map is stale: it says the plain-language → Option mapping ships empty and to pull it from the 2019 package; the 2026 edition files four rows.

Five things the census found, before any task runs

 Why it is new, not just different data
1The census overturns the plan's own premise. “Branch B is a stub” does not survive a direct read: every one of the 20 SetPremium rules already carries the full formula — IRPMFactor × PackageModFactor × Blkt<Form>AvgRate × (item value / BlktTotalFullValueAmount) × (BlktLimit/100). Building's Special and all four Contents forms reach it through an explicit Choose When on IncludedInBlkt = "Yes"; Building's other three forms reach it by elimination; Business Income, Special Class and SC Business Income reach it through a plain Else — their SetPremium is not even a Choose. Zero of 20 files stub it out. What's missing is input, not code
2The three-chunk finding (C6-28 / C7-25 / C8-14) now has a named cause. SetNonEQBlanketValues — one copy per premium group, five total, called from a dispatcher with two literal names (SetBlanketValuesSection on Building and SC Business Income, SetBlanketValues on the other three — Chunk 8's shared-name trap, generalizing to blanket's own machinery) — is the only rule that ever assigns BlktCauseOfLossValue a real value, and has three distinct ways to leave it short: no Else on the outer gate, an inner Else writing the literal "Not Applicable", and a ForEach lookup that silently no-ops with no matching UnitNumber. The rule was never broken. Nothing has ever fed it
3A wholly separate 621,894-character, 135-rule engine already exists, untouched by any chunk 1–8 script. CommercialPropertyBlanketRatingDetailRules.Rule.xml — 32 *Calc rules, 31 SetMax* rules, 30 *AgreedVal rules — computes the averaged and highest rate per coverage-form across every risk sharing a BlktIDNum. This is what actually writes Blkt<Form>AvgRate and BlktTotalFullValueAmount, the two inputs point 1's formula is starved of. Florida overrides this file
4ISO's own fixture corpus has never demonstrated a blanket submission either. Of 924 STC payloads, 364 carry IncludedInBlkt (almost always "No" boilerplate) and zero set it to "Yes" or carry any CommercialPropertyBlanketRating* record. A chunk-9 golden case will be the first populated blanket submission built anywhere in this project, with no ISO fixture to check it against
512 blanket-only Rate Tables exist, all but one a single countrywide row — BldgPrsnlPropBlktCoverageCode, four Blkt*RatingIdentificationCode variants, BuildersRiskBlanketFactor (1.05), CoinsuranceFactorBlkt (0.95), two Earthquake blanket risk-ID tables, three Flood blanket tables. Five states file their own substandard-class variant (DC, IL, KY, NJ, NY); three (AK, FL, PR) file their own Flood variant. None of the 12 has been read by any chunk 1–8 script

The state layer Only 4 state copies of the 20 premium files exist at all, across 2 jurisdictions (PR overrides Building Special) — consistent with chunk 8's finding that the core premium chain is the quietest layer in the corpus. State activity concentrates in the endorsement families instead (213 groups, 21 states), not in the five core SetPremium rules blanket depends on.

Gate-level tasks — 14 of 14 done, gate filed at 9 of 9

 #Task
✅C9-01Hand-derived $255/$510; the engine first returned $0/$0. Root cause: ForEach AtInputDataDef reparented WRITES onto the iterated Detail record along with reads, so the averaging engine's output never reached the structure — four stray Blkt*Temp fields proved it, sitting on the Detail node undeclared in its own DataDef. Fixed, user-authorized, 2026-09-21: Frame gained a write_root; an AtInputDataDef loop now keeps writes pinned to the pre-loop frame while still rebinding reads; AtDataDef loops (chunk 4's repeating group among them) are unchanged. Re-run matches hand-derivation exactly: $255 / $510. Every chunk 1–8 script and all 16 GL suites re-verified green. See CF-CHUNK9-GOLDEN-CASE.md §8
✅C9-02ErcDoBlanketRating is a 7-step Sequence, fanning out through ErcSetRiskLevelAverageAndHighestRates (21 further RunRules) and SetFinalCalculations (11 more). The task doc's Set*StructureCalc pair is real but sits 3 RunRule hops down, not a direct child — and a third rule it never named, SetMaxBasicGroupICauseOfLossAdjmtRate, is what actually feeds SetBasicGroupIAvgRate's Highest branch. Scaled the golden case to 3 structures, hand-derived first: rate 0.242, premiums $242/$484/$726, engine exact. ✅C9-03The election is live and genuinely different, on both sides. Same 3-structure case, flipped to "Highest": hand-derived rate 0.275, premiums $275/$550/$825, engine exact — Average stayed at 0.242. The finding: “Highest” means highest RATE, not highest dollar amount — despite sharing “StructureCalc” in the name, SetMaxBasicGroupIStructureCalc never multiplies by value, so with LOI falling as value rises, the smallest structure's rate governs the whole blanket group. No engine gap; both branches are live code

Six things the opening census found, before any task ran

 Why it is new, not just different data
1The population count holds; two of the build order's other three estimates do not. Rules/*Earthquake* is exactly 97 files, confirming the plan. But Special Class is 27, not “roughly 29”, and Legal Liability is 9, not 8 — and zero of the 97 filenames contain “LegalLiab” anywhere; all 9 were found only by reading each file's SetPremium branches. Non-Owned Trailers' count of 1 was the one estimate already exact
2The real rating engine is not in the 97-file population the build order points at. SetEQBaseRate, SetEQRate, SetFinalEQRate, SetEQCauseOfLossAdjustment, SetEQDeductibleFactor and four more all live inside CommercialPropertyStructureRules.Rule.xml and its four Contents/Business Income/Special Class/SC Business Income siblings — the SAME five parent files chunks 1–9 already opened, none of them named *Earthquake*. EQ's own SetPremium, inside the 97 files, is a wholly separate premium node that reads the already-computed rate and adds its own Product ToDataDef="Premium" on top — two separate premium trees on one submission, the opposite shape from blanket, which lived inside the ordinary SetPremium files themselves
3EQClass and EQTerr are pure submission inputs, the same shape as chunk 2's BasicGroupIISymbol. Neither is ever assigned by a rule anywhere in the countrywide corpus. EQTerr IS read (not written) by the location rules and each parent file's LookupEQLossCostBldg family, keyed State + EQClass + EQTerr — a real, distinct territory concept, just not a resolved chain the way Building's territory is
4EQ shares zero multipliers with the ordinary chain. IRPMFactor, PackageModFactor and MultiPremiumAndDispersionCreditFactor are absent from EQ's SetPremium entirely — it runs instead on EQDeductibleFactor, EQCoinsuranceFactor, EQPercentageDeductibleFactor, EQDeductibleByLocationFactor and 13 more sub-limit-tier variants, 57 distinct EQ-prefixed names total (C10-01 later found one exception: EQCoinsuranceFactor actually SHARES the ordinary CoinsuranceFactor table)
566 EQ-named Rate Tables exist; 12 are read by zero Lookup rule anywhere, including EQRateBldg and EQRatePrsnlProp — the two tables whose names most look like “the” EQ rate table, and are not. 17 of the 66 are header-only at countrywide
6ISO's own fixture corpus DOES demonstrate Earthquake — unlike blanket. 69 of 924 STC payloads elect EQCovIndicator to something other than “No Coverage”/“Not Applicable”, all 69 carry a populated EQClass. Chunk 10 does not face chunk 9's “no ISO fixture, ever” problem, and OI-99 is testable here in a way it wasn't for blanket

The state layer Two layers, diverging sharply. At the wrapper-file layer, 56 of the 97 *Earthquake* files have at least one state package copy somewhere in the 47 — file-existing, not rule-overriding. At the engine layer (the 5 parent files' EQ-prefixed rules), overrides are real but uneven: Building 10 of 47, Contents 9, Special Class 7, Business Income 5, and SC Business Income 0 of 47. FL and PR appear in every group that has any override at all.

Eight things the opening census found, before any task ran (2026-09-22)

 Why it is new, not just different data
1All seven of the build order's claims are true, but the file count overstates the rating surface by a third. There are 32 *LeaseholdInterest* files, but 11 do no rating at all. The FactorsFor<N>Percent files (5%–15%) declare only InitializeRuleSet, CalculateTotalPremium and ErcProcess: they are form-attachment markers with no Lookup, Premium or factor. The real rating surface is 21 files: 1 parent plus the 4×5 grid
2“Its own datadef group” is 32 groups, not 1: one per file, 21 of them rating-bearing. Gate points 6 and 7 are per-group questions, so chunk 11 asks them across 32 groups. Chunks 6–10 asked them across 5. This is the largest structural jump in the plan so far, and it is a count, not a difficulty estimate
3The fifth form is Earthquake. All 20 grid cells are filed, across Basic Group I, Basic Group II, Broad, Special and Earthquake. Chunk 10 is a direct input, and this is the first coverage opened after the form it depends on was built
4The rul:Power is real, unique and guarded. It appears in exactly 1 of 882 countrywide rule files, LI's own parent. It sits behind an IsNull guard on DiscFactorRaisedToNumOfMonthsRemainingAtInception, so a payload that supplies that value bypasses the annuity entirely. No ISO comparison would catch it, because ISO has the same guard
5Leasehold Interest is the exact inverse of Earthquake on shared machinery. EQ reads IRPMFactor/PackageModFactor in none of its groups (C10-14). LI reads both, in 16 of 32 files. Coinsurance, LOI, deductible and blanket are absent from all 32
6One table is dead on arrival. SpecialFormExclgTheftLeaseHoldBaseRate has 0 rows and no Lookup reads it anywhere. Its near-namesake ...IncldgTheft... has 12 rows and is live. This was caught at opening rather than mid-chunk
7Zero ISO fixtures. Of the 364 CF STC payloads (not 924; C10-20's correction), 0 mention Leasehold Interest. That puts this chunk in chunk 9's position, not chunk 10's: confirmation has to come from a live ISO call, and that call is planned from task 1 onward
8The state layer is the thinnest of any chunk so far. Only FL and PR file any LI file in their own package, and both carry a Set/Lookup rule. Every other state runs countrywide unmodified. A filed file is not the same as an overridden rule, so both layers still get enumerated

13 Terrorism / TRIA — a premium on every coverage, not a coverage of its own GATED 2026-09-24 — 30 of 30, 9 of 9

Opened 2026-09-24. 30 tasks numbered C13-01–C13-30 in CF-CHUNK13-TASKS.md, derived from the build order’s item 13 and the opening census (scripts/cf/143_chunk13_census.py). In plain English: terrorism is an extra premium charged on each coverage the policy already has, backed by a federal program (TRIA) that has an expiry date. So each coverage’s terrorism premium is split into before and after TRIA expires, and five exclusion or limitation forms can remove, cap or sub-limit it. “All states” means the 46; live ISO calls go at 2026-09-17 (OI-105).

What the census found (2026-09-24)

WhereCountWhat it is
Terrorism-named rule files (countrywide 2026)17 (build order said 16)13 form files with rules but no premium, 3 inert detail files, 4 with a SetPremium (3 disclosure forms and PostTRIA). Older parents (20230801 V01–V03) have 14
Terrorism rules embedded in core files108policy 23 (13 form attachments, 3 stat codes), location 9 (territory tier, loss costs), and each of the five coverage groups 11–19, each with its own SetTerrorismPremium, SetPreTRIAPremium, SetPostTRIAPremium
Rate tables6TerrorismLossCost, TerrorismTerritoryTier, TerrorismNBCExclFactor, TerrorismSubLimitFactor are header-only countrywide, filed by 44–46 states. Two stat-code tables, 1 row each
States that really change a terrorism rule20 of 46FL (32 rules), NY (34), AK (20, its own Alaska forms). 15 states file a "fire exception" factor: AZ CA CT GA IA MA ME MO NC NJ OR RI VA WI WV (Oregon, the golden-case state, is one). IL validates territory against ZIP/county. AZ files its own CP 09 01 amendment. CT, MA, VA rewrite the premium rules. CA, TX: CP 00 70 messages
PostTRIA coverage8 statesAK CA DC FL IL MD NY VA
ISO STC fixtures354 of 364 elect terrorism (TerrorismIndicator Yes)345 "TRIA in effect", 10 "will expire", 5 "already expired", 4 "expired, conditional forms". 11 carry a conditional exclusion, 6 a sub-limit above 0
Stored ISO responses14 carry a non-zero TerrorismPremiumnone of our comparers has ever looked at that field
Engine readiness by element nameevery element implementeddate arithmetic present: DateDifference (the OI-107 field), DateCreate ×2, DatePart ×6

Three things that shape the plan. (1) The engine has been computing terrorism all along, unchecked: every element is implemented, and 14 stored ISO responses already carry a non-zero terrorism premium nobody has compared, so they come first. (2) “Reads other groups’ finished premiums, so it runs last” looks wrong: each coverage’s terrorism premium reads its own record’s factors; C13-02 settles it. (3) Oregon files the fire-exception factor, so the first golden case proves a state mechanism.

Grouped like release candidates, in the order to do them: each group is a shippable step. gate tags name the points a task closes.

RC-1 · Free evidence and readiness (5) · COMPLETE 2026-09-24 Cheapest first, and it may reorder the rest: the evidence we already hold, and the engine risks, before any golden case.

 #Task
✅C13-01Opening census: named files, embedded rules, tables, states, fixtures, elements — Done 2026-09-24 (143_chunk13_census.py). Findings above
✅C13-02Rule order and dependency: where terrorism runs in ErcProcess/ErcRate, and whether anything reads another group's finished premium. Settles the build order's "runs last" claim — Done 2026-09-24 (144_chunk13_c13-02_rule_order.py, clean in all 5 parent editions). The claim is half right. Each coverage record computes its own Pre-TRIA, Post-TRIA and terrorism premium at the end of its own ErcRate, from its own factors only, so no coverage depends on another. But no coverage total includes that premium: a policy-level roll-up record (one of three disclosure forms, or PostTRIA) sums TerrorismPremium over every coverage record at every location, and that is what enters the policy total. The roll-ups run at steps 48–50 and 57, after the last location (41). The Zz prefix is how the forms sort last. CA, DC, IL, MD, NY and VA add a TerrorismCapStatePremiumCheck after the roll-up (→ C13-19/20)
✅C13-03Replay the 14 stored ISO responses with a non-zero TerrorismPremium through our engine, and compare the terrorism cells only. No ISO calls needed — Done 2026-09-24 (145_chunk13_c13-03_replay.py). Every per-coverage terrorism premium in all 14 agrees with ISO (Pre-TRIA, Post-TRIA, total; building, contents, business income; GA, MO, NC, NY, CA, AK, WY). 12 of 14 agree completely. The 2 that differ are CA's disclosure roll-up: ISO $153, ours $0, because CA's 25% cap reads the policy total through ancestor::CommercialProperty and our engine reads every ancestor:: as the root → OI-111 (engine change, awaiting the user's go). A named-ancestor reading, tested in the probe only (146_…), gives 14 of 14. Also found and fixed in the replay, not the engine: ISO sends PostTRIA as a single record the rules address without a table; the chunk 12 converter wrapped it, so AK's $20 and WY's $2 read as absent until the replay unwrapped rule-addressed singletons
✅C13-04Operand types over all 125 rules (17 named files + 108 embedded), with dates first: DateCreate, DatePart, DateDifference — Done 2026-09-24 (147_chunk13_c13-04_operand_types.py). Clean. 3,054 operators across 1,969 terrorism rules (5 parent editions + every state layer): every signature is one the engine handles, no undeclared type. Dates are confined to one rule, SetDaysUntilTRIAExpiration (DatePart ×6 → DateCreate ×2 → DateDifference, per edition). 14 Policy assembly — the total, the policy modifiers, statistical coding GATED 2026-09-24 — 22 of 22, 9 of 9

Opened 2026-09-24. 22 tasks numbered C14-01–C14-22 in CF-CHUNK14-TASKS.md, from the build order’s item 14 and the opening census (scripts/cf/156_chunk14_census.py). In plain English: chunks 1–13 priced every piece of a policy; chunk 14 adds them up, applies the whole-policy adjustments (IRPM, package modification, multi-premium credit) and checks the statistical codes ISO attaches to every record.

What the census found (2026-09-24)

WhereCountWhat it is
The total881 of 882 rule files carry a CalculateTotalPremium; 130 rules write ErcCalculatedTotalPremiumthe policy's own CalculateTotalPremium sums 45 record types (locations, the terrorism roll-ups, policy forms), and ErcCalculateTotalPremium writes the policy Premium
Minimum premiumnone in our 46 statesthe countrywide MinimumPremium and PolMinPremium tables are header-only, and no rule in any package reads them. The only minimum-premium logic in the whole corpus is Puerto Rico's (a residential condominium earthquake endorsement), which is out of scope. ISO's Property service does not apply a minimum premium: it prices a policy, it doesn't administer one
Policy modifiersIRPM 6 rules, package modification 1, multiple-location average rates 1, Standard Property Policy 4, SPP eligibility 1, additional-location reset 1IRPM: a policy form with TotalModificationPct, state maximum credit / debit tables, and a "cannot exceed 25%" message
Statistical coding486 stat-code rules (138 dispatchers), 34 tablesa stat code on nearly every record
State layerthe policy total overridden in 41 / 43 states; IRPM in NE only; the stat-code dispatcher in FL onlythe total overrides are likely pass-throughs that add state-only records; C14-04 checks
Evidence already stored293 ISO fixture responses (chunk 13), every one carrying the policy ErcCalculatedTotalPremium; 10,633 stat-code valuesa first comparison needs no new ISO call

Two things that shape the plan. (1) Minimum premium is out of ISO’s scope: the build order listed it, the corpus doesn’t rate it (C14-03 records it as a company rule). (2) The evidence is already on disk: 293 stored ISO responses carry the policy total and 10,633 stat codes, so RC-1 compares before any new call.

Grouped like release candidates, in the order to do them.

15 Manual-premium endorsements — the company enters the premium GATED 2026-09-25 — 21 of 21, 9 of 9

Opened 2026-09-25. 21 tasks numbered C15-01–C15-21 in CF-CHUNK15-TASKS.md, from the remaining-build plan and the opening census (scripts/cf/167_chunk15_census.py). In plain English: for these ten endorsements ISO publishes no rate. The underwriter types the charge in (ManualPremium); ISO’s rules only apply the policy-wide adjustments chunk 14 proved and round to whole dollars. The arithmetic is small; the work is making sure each one reaches the right adjustments, rounds the way ISO does, and is switched off in the states that switch it off.

What the census found (2026-09-25)

WhereCountWhat it is
Population23 countrywide premium files in 10 families (+3 container files with no premium: 2 Discretionary Payroll details, the Electronic Commerce parent)Cannabis Exclusion 10, Discretionary Payroll 2, Electronic Commerce 2, Limited International 2, Interruption of Computer Operations 2, and one each for Valuable Papers, Newly Acquired Location (time element), Newly Acquired Property increased limit, Portable Storage Units, BI Y2K

The formulaone, in four variantsManualPremium × package modification × multi-premium credit × IRPM. A: all four, 15 files. B: no multi-premium credit, 6 files (every Business Income and Special Class BI record). C: one multiplication of three operands, 2 files (Interruption of Computer Operations). D: same as A written factor-first (4 files); the innermost multiplication is still ManualPremium × package modification

Engine readinessevery rul: element registeredno date or Power arithmetic

Three things that shape the plan. (1) The risk is in the plumbing, not the arithmetic: one formula, but the modifiers are read through seven different relative depths, so RC-1 resolves every path before any golden case. (2) Rounding differs by variant (three roundings, two, or one), so RC-2 takes one golden case per variant, chosen so the variants would give different dollars. (3) The states act by switching off, not by rewriting: GA, TX and VA empty the rating step, FL forces the premium to $0; RC-4 confirms each live.

Grouped like release candidates, in the order to do them. gate tags name the points a task closes. RC-1 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (168_chunk15_rc1.py). RC-2 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (169_chunk15_rc2.py --live): engine 27 of 27 as derived, ISO 27 of 27, every premium on every policy equal (679), messages identical. RC-3 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (170_chunk15_rc3.py --live): 22 zero, exclusion and message cases, engine 22 of 22, ISO 21 of 21 rated, messages identical; ISO refuses a policy package discount of 0 (OI-103). RC-4 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (171_chunk15_rc4.py --live): all 46 states, ISO 46 of 46, every premium and message; states only switch families off (GA, TX, VA, FL, and MO through its 2023 V02 edition); Virginia 2021 explained ($927); OI-114 opened. RC-5 COMPLETE, chunk 15 GATED 9 of 9 on 2026-09-25 (CF-CHUNK15-GATE.md): the endorsements move the total by exactly their premiums and carry no terrorism or stat codes (clean test T2, ISO); regression CLEAN for chunks 0–15, 105 scripts; 157_ --all 293 of 293.

Opened 2026-09-25. 23 tasks numbered C16-01–C16-23 in CF-CHUNK16-TASKS.md, from the remaining-build plan and the opening census (scripts/cf/173_chunk16_census.py, carrying chunk 15’s two new checks: messages in every rule file, premium rules in every edition). In plain English: ISO's own example policies already switch these coverages on, and when chunk 14 re-rated them every premium matched. But those examples only cover part of each family. Of 43 premium records, ISO has priced 16 and never priced 27. This chunk proves the other 27 and the rest of each gate: every form, the zero cases, the messages, the state rewrites and the older editions. Most of these are Business Income options that price off the Business Income rate chunks 5 and 7 built (extended period of indemnity, no waiting period, a longer civil authority period). Two are location or contents coverages with their own small tables (pollutant clean-up, spoilage), one is a factor on the Group I rate (molten material), and Mine Subsidence is a different mechanism in each of the five states that file it.

What shapes the plan. (1) Every formula borrows a finished value from another record. The Business Income options read their parent's Final…Rate (chunk 5 / 7); Pollutant Clean Up reads the *highest* rate across the location's buildings; Molten Material reads the building's Group I rate and loss-of-income factor. RC-1 resolves every borrowed path on a real tree and checks it is computed before it is read (rule order), the way C15-03 resolved the modifiers. (2) The rates round twice. Every Business Income option multiplies at 3 decimals, then to whole dollars through the modifiers; Pollutant Clean Up and Molten Material chain several 3-decimal steps. Golden cases are chosen so the rounding points decide the dollar. (3) Two shapes are chunk 15's. Civil Authority's Radius form and three of Mine Subsidence's states (IN, OK, WV) are company-entered premiums, so they are cheap once the rest is proven. (4) Mine Subsidence is five mechanisms, not one: IL prices from tables (first $10,000 and each additional $10,000, by construction), KY from its own two tables, IN, OK and WV from a manual premium (OK through the policy modifiers). The census's table count missed KY's, IN's and WV's tables: they are filed under state names, not the countrywide ones (the standing check-sibling-tables rule). (5) Editions matter twice (the new per-edition check caught both): Missouri's 2023 V02 has a different Business Income EQ formula for Extended Period of Indemnity, and no Special Class Molten Material files at all. (6) Scope: Molten Material's Vacancy Permit riders stay with Vacancy Permit (chunk 18); MA's heating-oil pollutant credit stays with the state-only chunk (23).

Grouped like release candidates, in the order to do them. gate tags name the points a task closes. RC-1 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (174_chunk16_rc1.py, 123 checks): 37 stored ISO premiums equal ours, and a hand evaluation from the values each path reaches equals the engine and ISO on all 37. RC-2 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (175_chunk16_rc2.py --live): all 27 never-priced records, 49 predicted, engine 49 of 49, ISO 18 of 18 cases, every premium (2,311) and message equal; ISO confirms Pollutant Clean Up’s “highest” rate is the last building’s ($22, not $60). RC-3 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (176_chunk16_rc3.py --live): 32 zero, message, submitted-factor and exclusion cases, ISO 32 of 32; Pollutant Clean Up goes negative below $10,000; ISO honours a submitted factor as we do; an E&O record with no limit is refused by ISO (OI-103). RC-4 COMPLETE 2026-09-25 (177_chunk16_rc4.py --live): all 46 states and 6 probes, ISO 52 of 52, 8,342 premiums; Texas computes a Spoilage factor it never uses; FL and RI truncate Spoilage’s limit. ✅C16-09Pollutant Clean Up: the Broad form, and the "highest rate at the location" rule on a two-building location where the buildings' rates differ; ISO live — Done 2026-09-25 (175_, G9, G9b). Two buildings (class 0951 Broad, 100,000; class 0311 Special, 200,000), limit 60,000, deductible 2,500 (0.97): Group I $22, Group II $48, Broad $3, Special $3, EQ $4. The finding: the location's "highest" Group I rate is the LAST building's, not the highest. Each building's rate is its Group I adjustment × loss-of-income factor: 0.239 × 1.172 = 0.280 and 0.097 × 1.039 = 0.101. SetHighestBldgPollutantBGIRate binds the running highest as a WithArgs parameter once, before its loop, so every building beats the starting 0 and the last one wins: 0.101 → $22. I first predicted the maximum (0.280 → $60); the engine gave $22; ISO gives $22. G9b swaps the buildings, where both readings agree: $60, ISO agrees. Every premium and message equal on both

17 Business Income add-ons GATED 2026-09-28 — 23 of 23, 9 of 9

Opened 2026-09-25. 23 tasks numbered C17-01–C17-23 in CF-CHUNK17-TASKS.md, from the remaining-build plan and the opening census (scripts/cf/179_chunk17_census.py, carrying chunk 15’s two census checks: messages in every rule file, premium rules in every edition). In plain English: These six are options on a Business Income policy. They either narrow the cover (pay ordinary payroll for fewer days; deduct the power and heating bills the business saves while shut) or add a slice of cover (a landlord's lost rent, a mine's shutdown, a boat accident, a truck breakdown away from the premises). None of their 32 premium records has ever been priced by ISO in our stored responses. Unlike chunk 16, we have no ISO evidence to start from, so every record needs its own golden case. They price in four ways: - A slice of Business Income's own rate (payroll, power-and-heat): chunk 16's shape. The filed factors are 1.12 / 1.06 / 1.00 for 0 / 90 / 180 payroll days. If the premium uses (factor − 1), as chunk 16's containers did, 180 days prices $0. RC-1 checks this before any prediction. - The family's own rate table (Mining, Watercraft). - Landlord Rental Value runs its own rate chain. It reads the building's Business Income adjustments and base rates, not Business Income's final rate, and five states rewrite it. - Off-Premises Interruption is its own small rating plan: a loss cost for collision or other-than-collision, then a waiting period, extended cover and an insurance-to-exposure factor. Five states rewrite it and Florida switches it.

(2) Two families are chunk 16's shape. Ordinary Payroll and Power, Heat and Refrigeration read Business Income's Final…Rate × Factor × LimitToUse through the modifiers, like no waiting period. C17-04 reads the rule before this is assumed. (3) Two families price from their own rate. Mining's rate is by coverage option and cause, × the loss cost multiplier. Watercraft is Special-cause only: its factor by construction option × LCM × limit. (4) Landlord Rental Value is the hardest of the rate-driven five. It reads the parent's cause-of-loss adjustments, a type-of-risk factor (BusnIncomeFactor, 131 rows; FL's own), the Broad base rate and PolicyType, and the Special base rate with a theft exclusion. The census shows its Broad formula reads no loss cost multiplier: check that before predicting. Five states rewrite it, which is why the plan starts its golden cases here. (5) Off-Premises Interruption is a separate rating plan, with its own loss costs and up to seven factors, each rounded at 3 decimals. The other-than-collision form adds earthquake, flood and wind-hail endorsement factors that the policy's other coverages switch on. AR DC NY RI file their own endorsement factors; AK its own extended-BI and insurance-to-exposure tables and stat codes. (6) Editions and engine are clear. Every premium file exists and is identical in all four countrywide editions (so Missouri needs one confirming probe, not a special case), and every rul: element is already registered.

28 Engine behaviour decisions (each needs your go) Planned 2026-09-25 · size L

In plain English: places where our engine and ISO behave differently by design, or where a bad input silently produces a cheap policy. Each is an engine change, and none starts without your go.

FamilyFilesStates that rewrite itISO evidence already held
OI-103: ISO refuses where we price–about a dozen concrete cases; 2026-09-25: two more from chunk 15: a policy package discount of 0 (C15-11 Z5), and Florida’s special-class description table lacking a class our engine prices (C15-16); and one from chunk 16: a Mortgageholders E&O record with no limit (its rate lookup finds no row; C16-14)
Input validation: five inputs price at zero silently–AZ and others
A submitted derived field overrides the filed table–e.g. SpecialBaseRate
OI-113: minimum premium, refer to company–needs a referral layer
Housekeeping: a neutral home for the shared core–never bundled with a rating change

How it prices not a coverage; decisions about refusals, validation and referral.

Why here in the order last, because each is a design decision, not a trace, and the coverages above will add evidence (more refusals, more silent zeros).

Opens with OI-103: put the refusal cases in one table and propose a rule.

Open items that cross every chunk

ItemStatusWhat it is
A submission can populate a derived fieldNew, 2026-09-18Every IsNull-guarded rule honours a value that arrived in the payload — the guard cannot tell “already set by an earlier rule” from “supplied by the submitter”. A submitted SpecialBaseRate=9.999 bypasses the filed table entirely and prices at $11,398. The mirror image of the absent-means-zero family: it fails upward as readily as downward, and no ISO comparison catches it — ISO has the same guards and returns the same number
Input validationBiggest riskFive inputs price at zero rather than raising: Arizona's BasicGroupIReductionCredit, an unrecognised ConstructionType, an absent BCEG, a missing RatingTypeBasicGroupII, a missing Group II territory. Every one looks like a valid submission and silently produces a cheap policy
A missing date compared differently from ISO (OI-102)FIXED 2026-09-22A blank date falls back to 01/01/0001. ISO treats that as a real, very early date; our engine treated it as nothing, and a Leasehold Interest with no lease start date priced at $0 against ISO's $272. Fixed with authorization: in “before/after” comparisons the fallback now counts as the earliest date. ISO agrees live, and the regression is clean, 51 of 51
Date subtraction had the wrong sign (OI-107)FIXED 2026-09-22Our engine subtracted dates the wrong way round: ISO +257 days until TRIA expiry, ours −257 (confirmed live). Pro-rate factors were never affected, because they divide one difference by another. Fixed with your authorization; the Oregon case is now pinned in the regression
SingleResult with zero hitsOpen, narrowedISO refuses on some empty lookups and tolerates others; the distinguishing condition is unknown. 2026-09-22: one more refusal on record, LeaseholdInterestAnnuityConstant (OI-103). 2026-09-23: three more (chunk 12: EarthquakeDeductibleTier ×2, BuildingHeightFactor), each where we rate. Blanket strictness was measured and disproven. Needs targeted ISO probes, not more corpus reading
Gate point 9 — validation messagesOwed on C1 and C2A CF submission can rate correctly and still return errors; chunk 1 fires three on every case
Per-state gapsLoggedTX wind/hail untraced; DC and IL missing a BCEGFactor row; MT, NY, PR empty Group II symbol table
HousekeepingDeferredcf_engine imports gl_engine; the shared core wants a neutral home. Never bundle that rename with a rating change
General Liability, optionalA choice, not a defectWhether GL adopts propagate; whether it wants its own rounding tie (OI-70)
A Break inside a Sum (OI-108)FIXED 2026-09-23A blanket drone’s limit read 0 instead of 99,000 in CA, NC and MO, undercharging about $125. Confirmed live, then fixed with your authorization: the sum now finishes before the loop stops. Regression clean; live CA/NC/MO/GA 8 of 8 identical to ISO
Minimum premium: refer to company (OI-113)Backlog 2026-09-24ISO’s Property rating applies no minimum premium for our 46 states (empty tables, no reader). User decision: it is a company input, and the engine’s outcome should be “refer to company”, as GL’s ReferToCompany does. The Property engine has no company-input or referral layer yet; not built
A deductible-form message on older editions (OI-112)OPEN 2026-09-24In 5 older-edition fixtures (CA, MA, MO, NC) ISO clears three cause-of-loss answers on the multiple-deductible form and fires “Please select at least one Covered Cause Of Loss”; our engine keeps them. Message and echoed values only: all 177 premiums in those fixtures equal ISO. Found by C13-23; cause not yet traced
A named ancestor read as the document root (OI-111)FIXED 2026-09-24CF writes ancestor::CommercialProperty, ancestor::CommercialPropertyStructure and ~60 other record names (30,512 paths); our engine sends every ancestor:: to the root, the rule GL taught. Found by C13-03: California’s terrorism cap sees a $0 policy total (ISO $3,424) and prices terrorism at $0 (ISO $153). A named-ancestor reading, tested in a probe only, matches ISO on 14 of 14. Fixed with your authorization on 2026-09-24 (the 10th engine fix): the nearest ancestor with that name, the root only if none. Replay 14 of 14; regression CLEAN for chunks 0–12 and all GL suites; CA $153 pinned in the regression
A rul:Null inside a test, on old editions (OI-114)OPEN 2026-09-25Found by chunk 15 (C15-17): rating Virginia at 2021-06-01 (VA 20210501 over CW 20201201) stops in the building message DoMessageAtLeastOneBuildingOrPersonalPropertyRecordMustBeBlanketRatedToApplyMarginClause, where ISO itself replaced an Exist with Null (its comment: “Invalid path removed”); our engine refuses a condition that evaluates to nothing. No in-force package for our 46 states contains rul:Null (the 2026 countrywide edition has none), so no current rating is affected. Deciding how a Null test evaluates (presumably false) is an engine change: needs your go
A missing value in a premium (OI-109, OI-110)FIXED 2026-09-23ISO skips a missing value in a multiplication (live: $1, $3, $5 where “write $0” would give $0). A first change wrote $0 and was corrected the same day: Property now skips the null operand and records every skip; GL unchanged. Regression clean; 9 of 9 live probes identical; chunk 5’s Combined-risk case, a hidden divergence, now matches ISO ($256 + $30)

State coverage for chunks 9–12 lives inside their own checks, not in a premium sweep: blanket in 71_…state_override_census (0 of 47 override the averaging rules); Earthquake in 95_/96_; Leasehold Interest in 120_ (all 46 states); the six endorsement families in their gate scripts 128–142 (all 46 states, both layers). Live ISO confirmation for chunks 10–12 is in 118, 122, 129, 132 and 135; they are deliberately outside the regression and are re-run when a family or engine rule changes (Ordinance or Law was last re-run live on 2026-09-24, 8 of 8). Engine changes to date: ten, all user-authorized and ISO-confirmed — the chunk 9 ForEach reparenting, chunk 10’s banded lookup, chunk 11’s date comparison, OI-102, OI-104 and OI-107, chunk 12’s OI-108, OI-109 and OI-110 (two separate authorizations, the second correcting the first), and chunk 13’s OI-111 (named ancestors). Every one, in plain English with its technical notes: CF-ENGINE-FIXES.html. The separate checks above were run after the first nine; the one-command regression was re-run CLEAN after OI-111 (2026-09-24).

The habit that produced every finding

Derive the expected numbers by hand from the filed rows FIRST, write them down, then run. Every finding worth having in this project came from a prediction disagreeing with the engine — Arizona, the BCEG zero, the Kansas wind/hail mechanism, the cumulative forms. Observing what the engine does and writing that down proves nothing.
  1. The countrywide chain is often the minority behaviour. 39 of 47 jurisdictions override SetBasicGroupIIRate; 18 of 47 override the Group I chain. Re-ask per chunk, never inherit.
  2. Absent means zero, and nothing raises. The recurring failure shape in Property — which is why the zero cases exist in every chunk.
  3. A matching premium proves less than it looks. Nine cases matched ISO before a rounding tie was deliberately constructed, and every one was consistent with all three rounding modes.
